ParlaMint-AT_2005-04-05-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00102_d7e7719	The next item is the speech by Mrs Machne. Voluntary speaking time limit: 3 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-04-05-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00102_d7e7741	Madam President. Minister! High house! Ladies and gentlemen of the opposition, I don't know why you're so upset! In our Parliament, Members who have been elected freely and who know for themselves how to choose how to vote. I therefore believe that your advice is more than unnecessary for us. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] I would now like to turn to the issue of economics. Last year – but without your votes – we decided on a major tax reform. With the reduction of the KöSt, of course, group taxation is also a very important point on the way to a modern and competitive corporate taxation within Europe and ensures not only numerous jobs, but also corporate and holding locations in Austria. The concept of group taxation has been developed on the basis of many similar international regulations. For example, Denmark has already known the international offsetting of losses since 1903. Italy and France are already making up for international losses. ==References== Michaela Seiser writes in the “Frankfurter Allgemeine”: “With group taxation – it will cost 100 million euros in less revenue, according to estimates by the Ministry of Finance – Austria has a complete new country and could become a model for Germany.” As you know, Chancellor Schröder has announced his intention to take action in this regard. He is looking at the reform model in Austria. A proof of the forward-looking activity of Austria is the pending ECJ legal procedure of the British clothing chain Marks & Spencer. Their daughters have offices in Belgium, France and Germany, and these daughters have made losses between 1998 and 2001. The British Fiscus prohibited the offsetting of losses with domestic profits, and therefore the clothing chain moved to the UK Court of Justice, which rejected the claim but forwarded the case to the European Court of Justice. If this action is granted – and the judgment will take place in the next few days – this judgment will naturally affect all EU countries. By the way, the EU Commission has also supported the demand of Marks & Spencer. Against this background of development, it can only be stressed once again how forward-looking Austria operates here. To create jobs, to secure the future! That, ladies and gentlemen, is our motto.

In principle, the directive regulation of the European Commission and the proposed beneficiary of own resources for small and medium-sized enterprises is to be welcomed for up to € 1 million of loan and up to €50 million of turnover, but there are substantial suggestions from SMEs, which I would like to point out here. In Austria, around 90 percent of enterprises are micro-enterprises with one to nine employees. Due to the complexity and detail of the proposed regulatory framework and the resulting implementation effort in the banks, it is not unlikely that, in the case of micro and micro-credites, the benefits achieved in the own-funding scheme for small and medium-sized enterprises will hardly be felt. Small and medium-sized credit institutions, especially primary banks, are particularly important financing partners of SMEs in rural regions. At this point, I would also like to make an appeal to Austrian financial market supervision, which is called upon to keep the administrative burden on primary banks as low as possible. The Austrian Chamber of Commerce estimates the implementation of Basel II and the other directives for Austria at around €350 million. This significant administrative burden must not result in the local primary banks no longer or no longer being able to deal with the regional credit business of small and medium-sized enterprises for economic reasons, and perhaps the lending and decision-making at large banks' group headquarters should be carried out. A centralisation or further concentration of credit institutions and the associated acquisitions of small and medium-sized primary banks should not take place within the meaning of the broad economy. For small and medium-sized enterprises, the following should therefore be required: as simple as possible rating procedures for small and micro-credits, including improvements in credit collateral, movable real estate; and also the present draft of the Basel II agreement should give rise to appropriate value. Our economic and social system needs growth. Without new entrepreneurs – founders and contractors – it is difficult to increase growth. Many new entrepreneurs cannot set up and build up their businesses without credit. Therefore, the rules on the credit are of central economic and social importance.

I would like to refer to women's health today in particular. So far, women have often been excluded from many medical studies, so important gender issues have not been raised and this has led to a deterioration in adequate medical care for women in the long term. Only because women have a higher life expectancy does this not mean that women are healthier. Our Federal Minister has clearly stated this in the last week and has also said that something needs to be changed and something is being changed. Women have the right to the highest attainable standard of physical and mental health. The heart death is still the killer number one for women worldwide, except in sub-Saharan Africa. There, we know, AIDS is the most common cause of death. A study by the Boltzmann Institute of Women's Health, Tyrol, shows that every second Tyrolean dies a cardiovascular death, and every 26 years a breast cancer death. Women must first prove to be as hearty as a man to receive the same treatment. A comparison: again for Tyrol, Heart Death 1995 to 2000: The number of women who have died of a heart disease has increased by 9.5% over the period considered, and in men we see a decrease of 9.5%. So it turns out, it looks like the lifestyle of women has changed. This development cannot simply be accepted and cannot be explained solely with the increased life expectancy and smoking behaviour. The focus in medical education is on men. Sympathetics are aimed at men, and women are often diagnosed only later. Women also have other symptoms of heart disease. When it comes to nursing in the medical section, we learn very well that a heart attack can also express itself in stomach problems, and this happens more often. One could also say that women don't go to the doctor so fast, have others to do and are busy with their lives as far as children and family are concerned. There is currently no consideration for these differences in the training of physicians. You know, there are two models: Model A: six new permanent Security Council members and three still not permanently to do so. Model B: nine new non-permanent. From our point of view, from a democratic point of view, the model B, that is, nine non-permanent members, is much more welcome, because that would be a first step in actually democratizing the Security Council. These states would all have to vote in the General Assembly and say why they should be elected, what they intend to do, what positions they represent. As for the other model with the six permanent members – of the five that exist, we already know which positions they represent in about: The six new permanent members are fixed inside! A global and undifferentiated evasion of a whole country is certainly harmful. So this, Mrs Foreign Minister, is a number of tasks that we should take up. The focus of foreign policy on EU issues should not be a one-way road. I believe that small Austria – also in coalition with other smaller and medium-sized countries – can do a lot to help some of the vision we all have can be realized step by step from the vision of a common Europe.

From my point of view, there are in particular three points that are important to us and to which the Austrian delegation in New York also provides corresponding contributions. This is on the one hand the whole area of rule of law and good governance, on the other hand the comprehensive security concept that is important to us, because Vienna is, and should we not forget, the global security centre of the United Nations, the organizations that are located here and which are all concerned with the subject of security in different aspects.
